<note>The book features a traditional <term>leather binding</term> with <term>blind-stamping</term>—a decorative technique where patterns are impressed into the leather without the use of gold or color. The spine is constructed with five <term>raised bands</term>, which are the horizontal ridges formed by the cords used to sew the book together. A red leather <term>spine label</term> was originally used to display the title in gold leaf, though much of this gilding has been lost.</note>

# INQUIRY
### Concerning the
## <term>Hermetic Art</term>. <note>The "Hermetic Art" refers to Alchemy, a philosophical and proto-scientific tradition aimed at the purification and perfection of both materials (like turning lead to gold) and the human spirit.</note>

### IT.

### By a Lover of <term>Philalethes</term>. <note>Philalethes (Greek for "lover of truth") was the famous pseudonym of a 17th-century alchemist, usually identified as George Starkey, whose works were highly influential among scientists like Isaac Newton.</note>

### A Collection from <term>Kabbalah Unveiled</term>, <note>original Latin title: "Kabbala Denudata." This was a massive 17th-century work by Christian Knorr von Rosenroth that translated foundational Jewish mystical texts into Latin for a European audience.</note> and Translation of

### A
# A Short ENQUIRY
#### Concerning the
## *Hermetic Art.* <note>The "Hermetic Art" is a traditional term for alchemy, named after the mythical figure Hermes Trismegistus. It refers to the practice of seeking spiritual and physical transformation, most famously the turning of "base" metals into gold.</note>

**T**HIS Art, of bringing all Imperfect Metals to Perfection <note>In the alchemical tradition, "perfection" refers to the state of gold or silver, which were believed to be the most spiritually and physically "pure" forms of metal.</note>, has been asserted as truth by men of almost every rank, in most ages of the world; many of whose books still exist today.

They have declared that they have made and possessed this

many have been induced to believe and seek after this Art <note>The "Art" refers to the Hermetic Art or Alchemy—the ancient practice of seeking to transform base metals into gold and to find a universal cure for disease.</note>.

It is the sad observation <note>original: "Melancholy View"</note> I have taken of these men that has prompted me to organize my own thoughts into the order you find them, hoping that no master of the art will be offended, nor any seeker of knowledge <note>original: "Inquirer"</note> be displeased.

When I compare, I say, the variety of these men's fortunes, abilities, and other qualifications with those that the *Philosophers* <note>In alchemical literature, "the Philosophers" refers to the historical masters of the craft, such as Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Roger Bacon, who were believed to have successfully discovered the secrets of nature.</note> have established for men likely to succeed, it fills me with pity; it makes me almost tremble to repeat the words found in *Norton* <note>Thomas Norton (c. 1433–1513) was a renowned English alchemist. His famous poem, "The Ordinall of Alchimy," warns that the secret is a divine gift granted to very few.</note>, namely <note>original: "viz.", an abbreviation of the Latin "videlicet."</note>:

> *That of a Million, hardly Three*
> *Were ever destined for Alchemy <note>original: "Alchimy".</note>.*

<header>the Hermetick Art.</header>

O, what sad news for such men! Their failing health, ruined fortunes, and fruitless practice <note>The "practice" refers to the actual laboratory work of trying to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> make them less fit for the work every passing day. Instead of achieving the goal that should crown their hard work with success, they eventually risk rejecting, or even cursing, the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy or the Hermetic Art—the spiritual and chemical pursuit of perfection.</note> itself.

I wish to investigate quite thoroughly where this lack of success, which affects the majority of seekers, comes from. Accordingly, I will mention a few of the main obstacles <note>original: "Impediments"</note>, in my opinion.

*First*, only a few of those who seek this Art are qualified to achieve it, according to the *Philosophers* <note>In alchemical texts, "the Philosophers" refers to the ancient and medieval masters of the craft whose writings served as the primary authorities for later seekers.</note>; for they claim,

That, to find it, requires the whole Man <note>The "whole Man" refers to the necessity of dedicating one's entire being—intellect, soul, and physical effort—to the pursuit, rather than treating it as a part-time hobby.</note>; as well as that, when found, it possesses him: Also that it is never found by anyone by Chance, or by accidental trials <note>original: "Tryals"</note>, and casual experiments; and that unless the Mind be kindled with a Beam of Divine Light <note>In Hermetic philosophy, the "Divine Light" is a moment of spiritual grace or revelation from God that allows the seeker to understand secrets that cannot be learned through reason alone.</note>, it will not be able to penetrate this most hidden Science <note>The "hidden Science" refers to the Hermetic Art or Alchemy.</note>.

These, with many more Cautions, are plentifully set down in their books, on purpose to *inform* and *reform* a great many Persons too rashly concerned in these things; and yet how few take their Advice! undertaking this Study with much less than half the Man, constantly trying Experiments that have *no Authority but their own idle Fancy;* and

<header>A short Enquiry into</header>

# the Hermetick Art.

“ appointed his money to per-
“ dition <note>original: *perditionem*. In this context, it refers to the complete loss of one’s wealth and the ruin of their soul. The author is finishing a thought from the previous page: if you follow the literal instructions of alchemists, you will simply go broke.</note>.

By means of these seeming contradictions, bolder steps have been taken by some of them in discovering this Art <note>The "Art" is the *Ars Hermetica* or alchemy—the pursuit of the Philosopher’s Stone and the transmutation of metals.</note>, than otherwise they would have done, and even than some have dared to imitate, nay, so much as to repeat.

From hence I infer, That as much has been communicated to the world as can be expected, or that God will yet suffer to be discovered by writing. For this Art is declared, by those that have knowingly written of it, to be under his immediate protection <note>A common theme in alchemical literature is that the "Great Work" is a *Donum Dei* (Gift of God). No matter how much one reads, the final secret is only granted by divine grace to those who are morally worthy.</note>. ### the Hermetick Art.

"One book opens another," <note>original Latin: *Liber librum aperit*. This is a core alchemical maxim, suggesting that the "Green Lion" or "Philosopher's Mercury" might be explained in one text, while the necessary "Fire" is only explained in another, requiring the student to synthesize multiple sources.</note> says *Arnold* <note>Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a major medieval figure in medicine and alchemy.</note> the great Scholar;
*Anaxagoras* <note>A pre-Socratic Greek philosopher. In alchemical tradition, he was often cited for his theory that "everything is in everything," which justified the possibility of transmutation.</note> said the same for his *Work* <note>original: "Werk" - referring to the *Magnum Opus* or the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>,
Whoever is too lazy to look through many books,
Such a person will never be ready in his *Practice* <note>The actual laboratory work or "hand-operation" of alchemy.</note>.

The Reason he gives for thus *Reading* and *Comparing* many *Books*, is, that

Every single one of them taught but one or two points,
By which his fellow alchemists were made certain,
That he was a brother to them,
For every one of them understood each other.

# the Hermetick Art. 61

The *Philosophers* <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to the historical masters of alchemy, such as Hermes Trismegistus, Geber, or Paracelsus, rather than modern academic philosophers.</note>, you very well know, take the liberty of *seemingly Contradicting* themselves, and one another: Sometimes asserting the *Work* <note>The "Work" or *Magnum Opus* (Great Work) is the alchemical process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> to be *very easy*; other times, that 'tis *very difficult* or *hard*. One while, that 'tis *short*; then again, that 'tis *very tedious*. Again, that 'tis done with little *Expense*, and an *easy Labor*; then complaining of the *Charge* and *Toil*. Sometimes affirming their *Matter* <term>Matter (original: "Matter"): Also known as the *Prima Materia* or "First Matter," this refers to the fundamental substance an alchemist begins with to produce the Stone.</term> to be but *one only thing*, other times that 'tis *compounded* of *several*. One while the *Work* is to be done with a *gentle Fire* <note>Alchemists used "Fire" both literally (furnace heat) and metaphorically (the intensity of a chemical or spiritual reaction).</note>, another time that 'tis not *performed* without a *strong*. Then again, that 'tis *equal*, and of the *same degree*;

pure *homogeneal water* <note>A "homogeneal" (homogeneous) water is a solvent that is of the same essential nature or "root" as the substance being dissolved, allowing for a perfect union without adding foreign impurities.</note>, which
is its own *blood*, as white as
milk; which name some have
rather imposed. This *leprous* <note>In alchemy, a "leprous body" is a metaphor for an "unclean" or base metal, such as lead or copper, which is viewed as "diseased" and in need of healing or purification into "healthy" gold.</note>

<summary>The author clarifies that the alchemical "Green Lion" is not merely common vitriol, but a specific, powerful substance hidden within a base mineral. This "foul" matter must be purified and dissolved in its own "blood"—a homogeneous solvent—to reach its state of wonder.</summary>

**BEDIL** <note>Hebrew: "Tin." So if (1) be omitted, and you begin with (2), you will find in the sums the name *Bozez* <note>original: "Botzatz." This refers to a "shining" cliff mentioned in 1 Samuel 14:4. In Hebrew gematria (letter-numerology), *Bozez* is written as בּוֹצֵץ. When written "defectively" (without the letter *vav*), it sums to 182, which is exactly 175 + 7.</note>, as found in 1 Samuel 14:4, written defectively. ## C H A P. VI.

**WISDOM** <note>original: "CHOCHMAH," the second Sefirah on the Kabbalistic Tree of Life, representing the primordial point of creation and pure intellect.</note>, in the Metallic Doctrine, is the degree of **Lead**, or **Primordial Salt**, in which the **Lead of the Wise Men** <note>Also known as "Philosophical Lead." Alchemists believed this was a secret, "living" form of lead that served as the raw material for the Philosopher's Stone, distinct from the common, "dead" metal.</note> lies hidden. But how is so high a place attributed to Lead, which is such an ignoble metal <note>In traditional alchemy, lead was considered "ignoble" because it was heavy, dull, and easily tarnished, placing it at the bottom of the metallic hierarchy.</note>, and of which there is so seldom mention made in the Scripture?

But here lies Wisdom! Both levels are very secret; hence there is very little mention made of it. But yet here will not be wanting the number of the particular **Emanations** <note>original: "Sephirahs," the ten divine attributes through which the Infinite (Ein Sof) reveals itself.</note>.

For may not that which, in Zechariah 5:7, is called an **Exalted Talent** <note>The biblical verse reads: "And behold, there was lifted up a talent of lead." The author suggests this "lifted up" or exalted lead refers to the spiritualized state of the metal in the alchemical process.</note>

### Chapter VII. The Purifying Fire <note>original: *Æsch-Mezareph*</note>

9. **Zahab** <note>Hebrew: *zahav*, the word for gold</note>, or Gold, is called by the Name of the **Red Lion** <term>A symbol for the final, perfected stage of the alchemical process or the "Philosopher's Sulfur"</term>; and so not only do the least numbers <note>The "least number" is a method of reduction where the digits of a sum are added together until a single digit remains</note> of the Names **Labi** <note>Hebrew: *lavi*, a lion or lioness</note> and **Lish** <note>Hebrew: *layish*, an ancient or poetic word for a lion</note> make 14, which is the same Number that **Zahab** has; but also the least number of the word **Zahab** is 5 (as I just mentioned), which is equal to **Shachal** <note>Hebrew: *shachal*, a "Black Lion"; its value 338 reduces to 14, and then to 5</note>.

It is **Black in Color**, but **Red in potential** <note>original Latin: *in Potentia*; meaning it possesses the internal capacity to become red gold even if it appears black</note>.

# The Purifying Fire <note>original: *Æsch-Mezareph*; from the Hebrew *Esh Mezaref*, a treatise on the intersection of Kabbalah and alchemy</note>

But remember that all **Quicksilver** <term>Mercury</term> does not lead to this **Work** <note>The "Great Work" of alchemy, the process of achieving the Philosopher's Stone or spiritual perfection</note>, because they differ as **Flax** and **Silk**; but you work on **Flax** to no purpose, to make it receive the fineness and splendor of fine **Silk**.

And there are some that think this a sign of a **Legitimate Water** <term>The "Philosophical Mercury," a refined substance capable of dissolving metals</term>, if being mixed with **Gold**, it presently ferments and grows hot. But the common solution of **Mercury**, precipitated by **Lead**, performs this: And what will it do? <note>The author implies that while common mercury may react, it lacks the generative power required for true transmutation.</note>

Verily I tell thee, there is no other sign of a true **Mercury** but this, that in a due heat it invests itself with a ***Cuticula*** <note>Latin: "a small skin" or thin film</note>, which is the purest, finest **Gold**; and that in a little space of time, yea, in one night.

### Chapter VII: The Purifying Fire <note>original: *Æsch-Mezareph*; from the Hebrew *Esh Mezaref*, a treatise relating Kabbalistic principles to the alchemical art</note>

This is that which, not without a **Mystery**, is called ***Chochab*** <note>Hebrew: *Kokhav*, meaning "Star"</note>, a **Star**; because, according to the natural **Kabbala** <term>A system of Jewish mysticism; here used to find hidden alchemical meanings within Hebrew scripture</term>, Numbers 24:17 states: *Out of (the Metal) Jacob comes a Star*; or in plain terms, the figures of **Twigs**, and **Branches**, arise <note>Likely a reference to "dendritic" growth, where minerals crystallize into branch-like shapes, a sign to alchemists of "living" matter</note>; and from this **Star** comes or flows this **Influence**, of which we speak.

This **Quicksilver** <note>original: *Argent vive*, literally "living silver"</note>, in the **Gemara** <term>The section of the Talmud containing rabbinical commentaries</term> (Tractate Gittin, Chapter 7, folio 69) is called ***Espherica***, that is, **Spherical Water**, because it flows from the **Mundane Sphere** <note>The celestial or cosmic realm surrounding the Earth</note>.

### Æsch-Mezareph <note>Hebrew: *Esh Mezaref*, meaning "The Purifying Fire." This is a famous Kabbalistic-alchemical treatise that correlates the Sephirot (divine attributes) with metals and chemical processes.</note>

But remember that not all **quicksilver** <term>mercury</term> is suitable for this **Work** <note>The "Great Work" or *Magnum Opus*, the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, because they differ as much as flax and silk. You would labor in vain trying to make flax take on the delicacy and brilliance of fine silk.

Some believe it is a sign of **Legitimate Water** <note>A term for the "Philosophical Mercury" or the universal solvent sought by alchemists</note> if, when mixed with **Gold**, it immediately ferments and grows hot. Furthermore, this happens in a very short time—even within a single night.

### Æsch-Mezareph <note>Hebrew: "The Purifying Fire"; a treatise blending Jewish mysticism with alchemy</note>

All metals have their own origin: just as the nature of **Crown** <note>original Latin: *Kether*; the highest and most hidden of the divine emanations</note> is concealed, and from it all the remaining **Sephiroth** <note>the ten attributes or "spheres" of divine light in the Kabbalah</note> emanate. 

**Lead** holds the place of **Wisdom** <note>original Latin: *Chochmah*; the second Sephirah</note>, because just as Wisdom emerges most closely from the Crown, so Lead emerges immediately from the "metallic root." In other similar riddles <note>metaphorical or alchemical allegories</note>, it is called the "Father" of the following natures. 

**Tin** holds the place of **Understanding** <note>original Latin: *Binah*; the third Sephirah</note>, representing old age by its grayness, and foreshadowing severity and judicial rigor by its "cry" <note>original Latin: *stridore*; likely referring to the "cry of tin," a distinct crackling sound made when bending the metal, which the author links to the harshness of divine judgment</note>. 

**Silver** is assigned to the category of **Mercy** <note>original Latin: *Chesed*; the fourth Sephirah, representing loving-kindness</note> by all the Masters of the **Kabbalah**, primarily on account of its color and its use. 

And up to this point reach the "whitening" <note>original Latin: *albicantes*; substances or stages associated with the color white in the alchemical process</note> natures; now follow the "reddening" ones. And indeed, under **Sever—** <note>original Latin: *Gebhu-* (completing as *Gebhurah* on the following page); the fifth Sephirah, associated with power and judgment</note>

### Chapter I: Æsch-Mezareph <note>Hebrew: "The Purifying Fire"</note>

...thick <term>Kether</term> <note>the "Crown," the highest Sephirah, representing the first emanation of the Divine</note>, the Salt of <term>Chochmah</term> <note>Wisdom</note>, and the Sulphur of <term>Binah</term> <note>Understanding</note>, for reasons well known <note>referring to established alchemical theory that links these primary qualities to the highest divine powers</note>. And thus, the seven lower Sephiroth will represent the seven metals:

namely, **Gedulah** <note>Greatness, also called Chesed or Mercy</note> and **Geburah** <note>Severity or Strength</note> represent **Silver** and **Gold**; **Tiphereth** <note>Beauty</note> represents **Iron**; **Nezach** <note>Victory</note> and **Hod** <note>Splendor</note> represent **Tin** and **Copper**; **Jesod** <note>Foundation</note> represents **Lead**; and **Malchuth** <note>Kingdom, the lowest Sephirah</note> shall be the **Metallic Female** and the **Moon of the Sages** <note>original Latin: *Luna sapientum*; a symbolic term for the receptive, feminine principle in alchemy necessary for transformation</note>, and the field into which the seeds of secret minerals are to be cast—namely, the **Water of Gold** <note>original Latin: *Aqua Auri*; a reference to the biblical name *Me-Zahab*</note>, just as this name occurs in Genesis 36:39.

But know, my son, that in these things lie hidden such mysteries as no human tongue will be able to utter: *“However, I shall sin no further with my tongue, but I will guard my mouth with a seal,”* from Psalm 39:2.

### Æsch-Mezareph <note>Hebrew: "The Purifying Fire"</note>

<margin>*Kabbala Denudata* <note>Latin: "The Kabbalah Unveiled" by Knorr von Rosenroth</note>, page 235.</margin>
**GECHAZI** <note>Hebrew: *Gechazi*, the servant of the prophet Elisha</note> is the servant of Elisha, a figure representing common students of nature. These individuals indeed conduct contemplations regarding the valleys and depths of Nature, yet they do not descend into her hidden secrets. Because of this, they labor in vain and remain servants for eternity. They offer advice on how to procure the **Son of the Sages** <note>original Latin: *Filius Sapientum*; an alchemical term for the Philosopher's Stone or the perfected goal of the Great Work</note>, whose generation is impossible for nature alone <note>suggesting that nature requires the guided "art" of the alchemist to produce the Stone</note>.

<margin>2 Kings 4:14</margin>
But they themselves can contribute nothing to this generation—for which a man like Elisha <note>representing a true master or initiate</note> is required. Nature also does not open her secrets to them (Compare verse 26) and holds them in contempt (verse 30); and the restoration of the dead to life <note>symbolizing the alchemical "resurrection" or the animation of "dead" matter into the living Gold of the Sages</note> is impossible for them (verse 31). Hence, they are greedy (chapter 5, verse 20) and liars and deceivers (verses 22, 25) <note>referring to the biblical Gehazi who lied to the prophet for material gain, here used to describe fraudulent or "puffing" alchemists who seek only wealth</note>.

To **Nezach** <term>Netzach: "Victory" or "Endurance," the seventh emanation</term> belongs *Zahav Sagur* <note>original Hebrew: זהב סגור</note>, or enclosed gold (1 Kings 6:20–21; Job 28:15); namely, for the purpose of making feminine <note>original Latin: *ad feminificandum*; likely a technical alchemical term for preparing a substance to receive form or be "fertilized" in a philosophical sense</note>. To **Hod** <term>Hod: "Splendor" or "Majesty," the eighth emanation</term> belongs *Zahav Parvaim* <note>original Hebrew: זהב פרוים; a legendary, reddish gold mentioned in the construction of Solomon's Temple</note> (2 Chronicles 3:6).

# Æsch-Mezareph.

...the species of gold are placed; by one author in this way, by another in a different way. Now, however, I represent the nature of gold to you in **Tiphereth** <term>Tiphereth: "Beauty," the sixth Sephirah, located at the heart of the Tree of Life; it balances the polarities of the soul and is traditionally associated with the Sun</term>. Nor is there any reason for you to form objections based on the words of the *Zohar* <term>Zohar: "The Book of Splendor," the foundational text of Kabbalah</term> or the *Tikkunim* <term>Tikkunim: Likely referring to the *Tikkunei Zohar*, an influential set of commentaries on the beginning of Genesis</term>: for you must know that in this place, Tiphereth is understood in the mode of **Geburah** <term>Geburah: "Strength" or "Severity," the fifth Sephirah, representing discipline and judgment; in alchemy, it is often associated with fire and the metal iron</term>. 

And this is a great mystery, for Tiphereth commonly contains **iron** <note>original Latin: *ferrum*; in the planetary correspondence of the Sephiroth, Tiphereth is the Sun, but it is here being linked to the Martian qualities of iron/Geburah</note> within itself, and it is under this iron that we seek our **gold** <note>original Latin: *aurum*</note>. This is the **Sun** <note>original Hebrew: שמש (*Shemesh*)</note>, the Sun of nature and of art, whose "minor number" <note>A method of Gematria (alphanumeric calculation) where the digits of a sum are added together to find a core value</note> is 10, the symbol of all perfection. This number is also shown to you through Gematria by the minor number of that very level, **Tiphereth** <note>original Hebrew: תפארת; the numerical values of these letters (400, 80, 1, 200, 400) total 1081. In "minor" calculation, 1+0+8+1 = 10</note>; and the name **Eheyeh** <note>original Hebrew: אהיה; meaning "I AM," the Divine Name associated with the highest crown, but here linked by calculation to the perfection of the work</note> belongs to the same through a minor calculation. Mix therefore the iron and the clay, as in Daniel 2:33, and you will have the foundation of the golden summit—

### Chapter II. Æsch-Mezareph.

...of gold. # Chapter II. *The Purifying Fire* <note>original: "Esch-Mezareph"; a Hebrew-Latin title for this kabbalistic alchemical treatise</note>

# Chapter II. *The Purifying Fire* <note>original: "Esch-Mezareph"</note>

...of the **earth of the wise** <note>original Latin: *terræ sapientum*; referring to the refined prime matter of the alchemists</note>, for the entire duration of **Ezekiel's lying down** <note>A reference to Ezekiel 4:6, where the prophet is commanded to lie on his side for 40 days to bear the iniquity of Judah; in alchemy, this signifies a specific period of "digestion" or fermentation of the matter</note>.

### Æsch-Mezareph. 56

...are **Lavi** <note>Hebrew: לביא, a term for a lion or lioness</note>, which is **Lioness**, according to Job 4:11: *The whelps of the lioness shall be scattered* <note>original Latin: *Filii Leænæ separabunt se*</note>. Also Ezekiel 19:2: *Your mother was a lioness, she lay down among the lions.* And Nahum 2:11 <note>cited as verse 12</note>: *The lioness was there.* and verse 12 <note>cited as 13</note>: *The lion choked [prey] for his lionesses.*

Likewise there is **Laish** <note>Hebrew: ליש</note>, which denotes a **fierce lion** with a long and simple mane <note>literally "villus," referring to shaggy hair</note>, as is found in Proverbs 30:30: *A lion, mighty among beasts.*

These two names, by **reduced number** <term>Reduced number: In Hebrew Gematria, this is the "Mispar Katan" or "small number," where the digits of a word's total value are added together until a single digit remains (e.g., 43 becomes 4+3=7)</term>, relate to the **number seven**. For **Lavi** <note>L=30, B=2, Y=10, A=1</note> contains 43, which is 7; and **Laish** <note>L=30, Y=10, Sh=300</note> totals 340, which is likewise 7.

To these is equivalent the name **Phuk** <note>Hebrew: פוך</note>, or **stibium** <note>A mineral, antimony trisulfide, used in antiquity as black eye makeup and by alchemists as a "devouring" agent to purify gold</note>, which yields the total sum of 106, and by reduced number, 7. Nothing clearer could be said on the matter: especially if one considers the epithets given to that mineral, when it is called the **servant of the hair** or of the shaggy manes <note>This connects the "shaggy-maned" lion Laish directly to the mineral Antimony (Stibium) through their shared numerical value</note>.

<header>CHAPTER VII. THE PURIFYING FIRE <note>original: Æsch-Mezareph</note></header>

...[useful for] metals and **leprous minerals** <note>In alchemy, "leprous" refers to base or impure metals that require "healing" or purification to become perfect gold.</note>. This water flows from a double source: one is called **Jeor** <note>original: יאר / Jeor, meaning a stream or river</note> because of its fluidity, possessing a "right-hand" and benign nature <note>In Kabbalistic thought, the right side of the Tree of Life represents Mercy (Chesed) and expansion.</note>; the other is called **Dan** <note>original: דן / Dan, meaning "judgment"</note>, which is rigorous and of a harsh nature <note>The "left side" represents Severity (Gevurah) or judgment.</note>. It flows through the **Salt Sea** <note>The Dead Sea</note>, which should be noted, and is finally thought to be mixed with the **Red Sea**; this is the **sulfurous matter**, masculine in nature, known to all true workers <note>A traditional term for practicing alchemists, often called "Artisans" or "Philosophers."</note>.

Know also that the name **Zaku** <note>original: זכו / Zaku, meaning "purity"; numerical value: Zayin (7) + Kaf (20) + Vav (6) = 33</note>, when multiplied by 8—the number of **Jesod** <note>original: יסוד / Yesod, meaning "Foundation," the ninth Sephirah on the Tree of Life. In this specific mathematical context, the author correlates it with the number 8.</note>—produces the number 264, which is the value of **Seder** <note>original: סדר / Seder, meaning "order" or "arrangement"</note>. This same number is also contained in the word **Jarden** <note>original: ירדן / Jordan; Yod (10) + Resh (200) + Dalet (4) + Nun (50) = 264</note>; thus, you should remember that at least eight orders of purification are required before true purity follows.

*The Purifying Fire*, chapter 7.

Where you have the total sum of 260 from bottom to top, from right to left, and along the diagonal. Its minor number <note>The "minor number" or digital root is found by adding the digits of a number together; here 2+6+0 = 8</note> is 8, which is the number of **Yesod** <term>Yesod: The "Foundation," the ninth Sephirah on the Kabbalistic Tree of Life. The word translates to "departed" or "turned aside"</note>, meaning "it departed," because as you proceed, the sum always recedes in its variations:

For example: if you begin from 2, the sum will be 268, which is resolved into 7 <note>2+6+8 = 16, and 1+6 = 7. This resolution is the process of finding the digital root</note>. By beginning from 3, the sum will be 276, which is resolved into 6 <note>2+7+6 = 15, and 1+5 = 6</note>, and so on. For in this same way, as the number of **purifications** increases, the **weight of your water** <note>original Latin: *pondus aquæ tuæ*</note> decreases.

— *The Purifying Fire*, Chapter 7.

<margin>Dove <note>original Hebrew: יונה (*Yonah*)</note>.</margin>

Namely, **sulphur** with **salt**, Deuteronomy 29:23. **Sulphur** with **fire**, raining down upon the wicked—that is, the unclean metals—Psalm 11:6. This **Sulphur** must be dug up by you; yet you shall dig it out of water, so that you may have fire out of water: and if your way be right before the Lord, the **iron** shall swim for you upon the water, 2 Kings 6:6. Go, therefore, to the Jordan with Elisha (ibid. verse 4). But who can express the <term>Gebhuroth</term> <note>Hebrew for "mighty acts" or "powers," referring here to the manifestations of the Sephirah Gevurah</note> of the Lord? Psalm 106:2. Many seek out other Sulphurs; and he who has entered the "house of the particular paths," Proverbs 8:2, will understand. For the Sulphurs
